## Summary: IAM Architecture & Phased Implementation

I've reviewed all four IAM planning documents in full detail. Here are the complete findings:

### Core Architecture

**Identity Resolution + Authority Model (Two Pillars)**

1. **Person Entity** — Single canonical node with multiple external identities (GitHub, Slack, Google, MCP, platform). Each identity has provider_id, display_name, email, oauth_token, refresh_token, and token_expires_at.

2. **Authority Scopes** — Per-agent-type permission table with actions matrix (create_decision, confirm_decision, create_task, etc.) and constraints. Permission levels: **auto** (done immediately), **provisional** (draft, needs review), **propose** (needs approval), **blocked** (forbidden).

3. **Workspace Permissions** — Role-based (owner, admin, member, viewer) with optional project-level access granularity.

### Identity Resolution Chain (Staged)

- **Phase 1:** Exact provider match + email cross-provider match only
- **Phase 2+:** Display name fuzzy matching + multi-source (Slack, Google Workspace) resolution

### Two Agent Auth Patterns

| Pattern | Examples | Auth Method | Token Owner |
|---------|----------|-------------|------------|
| **Platform-managed** | Architect, Observer, PM agents | API key (Phase 1) → OAuth (Phase 2) | Workspace |
| **User-local** | Claude Code, Cursor, Aider | OAuth via `brain init` | Person + client registration |

### Permission Matrix (5 Agent Types)

| Action | Code | Architect | Management | Design | Observer |
|--------|------|-----------|-----------|--------|----------|
| create_decision | prov | prov | prov | prov | ✗ |
| confirm_decision | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| create_task | auto | auto | auto | prov | ✗ |
| complete_task | auto | ✗ | auto | ✗ | ✗ |
| create_observation | auto | auto | auto | auto | auto |

### Phase 1 (MVP—Ship Now)

**Done:**
- Person table with auth fields (contact_email, email_verified, image, updated_at)
- better-auth + SurrealDB v2 adapter integration
- GitHub OAuth (sign-in, account linking)
- 17 integration tests passing

**Remaining:**
- Add `authority_scope` and `workspace_permission` tables to schema
- MCP server API key validation (simple, workspace-scoped)
- Identity resolution (exact provider + email match)
- Platform-managed agent auth (hardcoded defaults)
- Extraction pipeline name resolution

### Phase 2 (Pre-Early Access)

**OAuth 2.1 for MCP Authentication:**
- Replace API keys with OAuth 2.1 (scoped, token lifecycle, DCR support)
- Recommended: Better Auth + OAuth Provider plugin (TypeScript-native, DCR, PKCE, JWT/JWKS, `/.well-known`)

**User-Local Agent Auth (`brain init`):**
- Browser-based consent flow → token with person identity + client registration
- Scopes: `graph:read`, `decision:create_provisional`, `task:create`, `observation:create`, etc.
- Short-lived access + 30-day refresh window
- `human_present` flag distinguishes interactive confirmation from autonomous decisions

**Authority Scope Configuration UI:**
- Allow workspace owners to customize per-agent permissions per workspace

**Multi-Source Identity Resolution:**
- Slack identity resolution (Slack user → Person via email)
- Google Workspace OAuth
- Display name fuzzy matching (candidates surfaced for manual linking)

### How Phases Relate

- **Phase 1 → Phase 2:** API key → OAuth 2.1 validation logic swaps but authorization layer unchanged
- **Identity resolution cascades:** Exact match (Phase 1) → email cross-provider (Phase 1) → fuzzy name + multi-source (Phase 2)
- **Permission matrix hardcoded in Phase 1**, configurable in Phase 2
- **MCP server token flow:** OAuth scopes map to authority actions; same permission checks whether token comes from API key or OAuth bearer token

### Key Implementation Files (Schema)

```sql
-- authority_scope: agent_type, workspace, actions{}, constraints{}
-- workspace_permission: person, workspace, role, project_access[]
-- person.identities[]: provider, provider_id, email, oauth_token, refresh_token
-- session, account, verification tables (better-auth)
```

**Knowledge base indexed as:** IAM: Unified Auth Layer, IAM Phase 1: MVP, IAM Phase 2: Pre-Early Access

Perfect! I now have comprehensive coverage. Let me create a final summary document based on all findings:

## Authentication & Authorization Implementation Summary

Based on thorough exploration of the codebase, here are the complete findings:

---

### 1. **McpAuthResult Type** (`app/src/server/mcp/auth.ts`)
```typescript
export type McpAuthResult = {
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  workspaceName: string;
};
```
**Purpose**: Returned after successful Bearer token verification. Contains only workspace context, no user/person record.

---

### 2. **Chat Authentication Flow**
- **No session validation** currently implemented in chat handler
- Chat agent inherits context via `ChatToolExecutionContext` passed through `experimental_context`
- `workspaceOwnerRecord` optionally loaded via `getWorkspaceOwnerRecord()` query but **not enforced as authentication**
- No user/person record required; chat is workspace-scoped only
- Better-auth tables exist (session, account, verification) but not wired to chat ingress

---

### 3. **ChatToolExecutionContext Type** (`app/src/server/chat/tools/types.ts`)
```typescript
export type ChatToolExecutionContext = {
  actor: "chat_agent" | "mcp" | "pm_agent" | "analytics_agent";
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  conversationRecord: RecordId<"conversation", string>;
  currentMessageRecord: RecordId<"message", string>;
  latestUserText: string;
  workspaceOwnerRecord?: RecordId<"person", string>;
};
```
**Actor usage**:
- Passed to `experimental_context` in `ai` SDK's `streamText()` call (chat handler line 84)
- Available to all tools via `requireToolContext(options)` helper
- Not currently used for permission checks; informational only

---

### 4. **Workspace API Key Hash Mechanism** 
Location: `workspace` table schema (line 55)
```sql
DEFINE FIELD api_key_hash ON workspace TYPE option<string>;
```

**Workflow**:
1. `POST /api/mcp/:workspaceId/auth/init` generates raw key via `generateApiKey()` (format: `brain_<32-hex-bytes>`)
2. Key hashed with bcrypt (cost=10) via `hashApiKey()` before storage
3. MCP requests verified via Bearer token: `verifyApiKey(rawKey, storedHash)`
4. On mismatch: 401 "invalid API key"; on missing config: 403 "workspace has no API key configured"

---

### 5. **Authority/Permission Code**
**Currently NOT implemented in production code**, but documented in IAM phase plan (`AGENTS.md`):
- `authority_scope` table (proposed, not yet in schema)
- `workspace_permission` table (proposed, not yet in schema)
- Hardcoded defaults planned per agent type (code_agent, architect, management, design_partner, observer)
- Function `fn::check_authority()` designed but not deployed

**Current enforcement**: Only workspace scoping via `isEntityInWorkspace()` and `requireScopedRecord()` checks in MCP routes.

---

### 6. **Person Table Schema** (lines 252-267)
```sql
DEFINE TABLE person SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD name ON person TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD contact_email ON person TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD email_verified ON person TYPE bool DEFAULT false;
DEFINE FIELD image ON person TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD identities ON person TYPE option<array<object>>;
DEFINE FIELD identities[*].provider ON person TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD identities[*].id ON person TYPE string;
```

**Identities structure**: Array of OAuth provider links with:
- `provider`: OAuth provider ID (e.g., "github", "google")
- `id`: Provider-specific account ID

---

### 7. **Better-Auth Integration** (migration 0010)
**Tables defined** (not yet integrated to chat):
- `session`: person_id + token (UNIQUE) + expires_at + ip/ua
- `account`: person_id + provider_id + account_id + tokens/scope/id_token/password
- `verification`: identifier + value + expires_at (for email/2FA codes)

**Current status**: Schema exists but only MCP uses API keys; web chat has no session validation.

---

### 8. **Workspace Membership** (graph relation)
```sql
DEFINE TABLE member_of TYPE RELATION IN person OUT workspace SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD role ON member_of TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD added_at ON member_of TYPE datetime;
```

**Only usage**: `getWorkspaceOwnerRecord()` queries `member_of WHERE role = 'owner'` to find workspace owner (optional context for chat agent).

---

### Key Takeaways
- **MCP is stateless**: API key → workspace only
- **Chat has no auth**: Uses workspace context only; workspaceOwnerRecord informational
- **Actor type**: Available for future permission checks but not enforced
- **Authority system**: Planned but not in schema/code yet
- **Session/OAuth**: Schema ready but not wired to chat ingress
- **Workspace scoping**: Only current hard boundary (all entities checked via `isEntityInWorkspace()`)

**Knowledge base sources indexed**: MCP auth module, chat handler, schema definitions, better-auth migration, permission checking patterns.

Here's how they connect:

## Phase 1 → Phase 2 Integration Architecture

### The Core Insight

Phase 1 builds two independent dimensions of authorization. Phase 2 adds richer identity sources and a configuration UI, but the enforcement layer stays the same.

| Dimension | Phase 1 | Phase 2 |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| **What can this agent TYPE do?** | `authority_scope` table, hardcoded defaults | Same table, workspace-specific overrides via UI |
| **What can this PERSON do?** | `member_of.role` (owner/admin/member/viewer) | Same, plus project-level granularity |
| **Who is this request from?** | API key → workspace + hardcoded agent_type; Session cookie → person | OAuth 2.1 token → workspace + person + agent_type + scopes |

### Critical Phase 1 Design Decisions

**1. `authority_scope` must have an optional workspace field**

```
Phase 1 seed:  (code_agent, create_decision, provisional, workspace=NONE)  ← global default
Phase 2 UI:    (code_agent, create_decision, blocked, workspace=ws:xyz)    ← override
```

Lookup: workspace-specific row → global default → blocked. No schema migration needed for Phase 2.

**2. Don't need a `workspace_permission` table**

`member_of` already has `role`. Phase 1 uses it for "is this person an owner?". Phase 2 extends it for role-based access. No new table.

**3. `check_authority` takes agent_type as input, not auth method**

```
check_authority(agent_type, action, workspace) → auto | provisional | propose | blocked
```

Phase 1: agent_type is hardcoded (MCP → "code_agent", chat → "chat_agent")
Phase 2: agent_type comes from OAuth client registration in a `connected_agent` table

The function doesn't change. Only how agent_type is resolved changes.

**4. AgentIdentity type must be extensible**

```typescript
type AgentIdentity = {
  agent_type: "code_agent" | "architect" | "management" | "design_partner" | "observer";
  human_present: boolean;
  workspace: RecordId<"workspace">;
  person?: RecordId<"person">;     // Phase 1: set for web chat, undefined for MCP
                                    // Phase 2: set for user-local agents too (brain init)
};
```

### How Each Phase 2 Feature Lands

**OAuth 2.1 for MCP** — Swaps the auth resolver (API key → OAuth token), but `check_authority` is unchanged. New: `connected_agent` table maps client_id → agent_type.

**`brain init` browser flow** — New auth path: browser OAuth → consent → scoped token. Token carries person_id + workspace_id + granted_scopes. Authority check adds: `granted_scopes ⊇ requested_action` as an additional gate.

**Slack/Google identity** — Just more providers in `person.identities[]`. Identity resolution chain extends, schema unchanged.

**Authority config UI** — CRUD on `authority_scope` rows with workspace field set. Phase 1's nullable workspace field makes this a pure UI addition.

**Connected agent management** — New `connected_agent` table + OAuth client credential lifecycle. Maps to existing `authority_scope` per agent_type.

### What Phase 1 Must Get Right

1. **`authority_scope.workspace` is nullable** — global defaults vs workspace overrides
2. **`check_authority` is auth-agnostic** — takes agent_type, doesn't know how it was resolved
3. **No separate `workspace_permission` table** — reuse `member_of.role`
4. **Tool enforcement calls a single function** — don't inline permission logic in 16 tool files
5. **`AgentIdentity.person` is optional** — MCP has no person in Phase 1, gets one in Phase 2

I have completed a thorough exploration of the tool execution surface. Here's the comprehensive analysis:

## Key Findings

### 1. **Actor Authority Type System**
**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/chat/tools/types.ts`

```typescript
export type ChatToolExecutionContext = {
  actor: "chat_agent" | "mcp" | "pm_agent" | "analytics_agent";
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  conversationRecord: RecordId<"conversation", string>;
  currentMessageRecord: RecordId<"message", string>;
  latestUserText: string;
  workspaceOwnerRecord?: RecordId<"person", string>;
};
```

The `actor` field carries 4 distinct roles, but **enforcement is minimal**.

### 2. **Authority Enforcement (ONE Tool Only)**
**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/chat/tools/confirm-decision.ts`

Only `confirm_decision` explicitly checks actor:
```typescript
if (context.actor !== "chat_agent") {
  throw new Error("confirm_decision is only available for chat_agent context");
}
```

**All other tools skip actor checks entirely.**

### 3. **Where Actor is Set**
**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/chat/handler.ts`

Chat handler hardcodes actor to `"chat_agent"`:
```typescript
experimental_context: {
  actor: "chat_agent",
  workspaceRecord: input.workspaceRecord,
  conversationRecord: input.conversationRecord,
  currentMessageRecord: input.currentMessageRecord,
  latestUserText: input.latestUserText,
  ...
}
```

PM agent and analytics agent compose shared tools but don't set their own `actor` values when invoking.

### 4. **MCP Route Bypasses Tool Layer Entirely**
**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/mcp/mcp-route.ts`

MCP endpoints call database functions **directly**:
- `handleCreateDecision()` calls `createDecisionRecord()` directly (not via tool)
- `handleLogObservation()` calls `createObservation()` directly (not via tool)
- `handleAskQuestion()` calls `createQuestionRecord()` directly (not via tool)

**Zero actor validation** at MCP layer — only API key authentication via `authenticateMcpRequest()`.

### 5. **Tools Missing Actor Checks (All Write Tools)**
Based on tool registration in `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/chat/tools/index.ts`, these write tools have **no actor validation**:

- `create_provisional_decision` — writes decision, no check
- `create_observation` — writes observation, no check
- `create_suggestion` — writes suggestion, no check
- `create_question` — writes question, no check
- `acknowledge_observation` — mutates observation, no check
- `resolve_observation` — mutates observation, no check
- `resolve_decision` — mutates decision, no check
- `create_work_item` — writes task/feature, no check

Only **read tools** and `confirm_decision` exist without mutation concerns.

### 6. **PM Agent Composition**
**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/agents/pm/tools.ts`

PM agent composes shared tools:
```typescript
export function createPmTools(deps: ChatToolDeps) {
  return {
    search_entities: createSearchEntitiesTool(deps),
    get_project_status: createGetProjectStatusTool(deps),
    create_observation: createCreateObservationTool(deps),
    create_suggestion: createCreateSuggestionTool(deps),
    suggest_work_items: createSuggestWorkItemsTool(deps),
    create_work_item: createCreateWorkItemTool(deps),
  };
}
```

**None of these check if the caller is `pm_agent`.** The PM agent invokes them, but when those tools run, `context.actor` is still whatever was set by the parent (chat_agent), not `pm_agent`.

### 7. **MCP Auth Result Type**
**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/mcp/auth.ts`

```typescript
export type McpAuthResult = {
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  workspaceName: string;
};
```

**No actor field.** MCP is treated as a monolithic `"mcp"` actor with API key auth, not fine-grained.

## Authority Enforcement Gap Summary

| Layer | Check Type | Coverage | Gap |
|-------|-----------|----------|-----|
| **Chat tools** | Actor via context | 1/15 tools (confirm_decision) | 14 tools missing checks |
| **PM agent tools** | Actor validation | None | All tools reuse parent actor |
| **MCP routes** | API key only | Yes | No per-action authority scoping |
| **Tool context helper** | Malformation check | Yes | Only validates existence, not authorization |

**Critical vulnerability:** Any tool composed by PM agent or MCP can write to the database without actor-based authorization.

## Authentication & Identity Infrastructure - Thorough Exploration

### Actions Taken
1. Gathered 10 key files covering auth config, adapters, chat routes, schema, and migrations
2. Indexed 27 sections with 85.2KB of content 
3. Searched for session validation, middleware, OAuth linking, and access control patterns

---

### Key Findings

**Session & Account Schema (migration 0010_better_auth_tables.surql):**
- `session` table: token (unique index), person_id (FK), expires_at, ip_address, user_agent, timestamps
- `account` table: person_id (FK), provider_id + account_id (indexed together), access/refresh tokens, token expirations, scope, id_token
- `verification` table: for email verification flow
- Person extends with: contact_email (unique), email_verified, image, identities (array<object> with provider/id fields)

**better-auth Configuration (auth/config.ts):**
- Custom SurrealDB v2 adapter (non-standard field mapping: contact_email ↔ email, person_id ↔ userId, email_verified ↔ emailVerified)
- GitHub OAuth provider configured (client ID/secret from env)
- Session/account/verification tables wired to person table
- Adapter enforces field name translation layer between better-auth defaults and project schema

**SurrealDB Adapter (auth/adapter.ts):**
- Uses SDK v2's `RecordId` for record references
- Implements `findOne()` returning `null` (boundary exception to project no-null rule)
- Foreign key map built dynamically from schema to resolve references
- Handles null comparisons with SurrealDB `IS NONE` / `IS NOT NONE` syntax
- Supports all where operators: eq, ne, gt, gte, lt, lte, in, not_in, contains, starts_with, ends_with
- CREATE returns AFTER for relation compatibility

**Chat Route Authentication GAP:**
- Chat ingress (`chat-ingress.ts`) receives POST to `/api/chat/messages` with workspaceId + conversationId
- **NO session/token validation observed in chat routes** — routes accept any workspaceId parameter without auth middleware
- Person resolution in extraction (`extraction/person.ts`) uses `findWorkspacePersonByName()` — searches by name within workspace, not authenticated user identity
- Chat processor loads workspace context but does NOT validate requester's workspace membership

**Runtime Config (config.ts):**
- Required env vars: `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID`, `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET`
- Surreal credentials: `SURREAL_NAMESPACE`, `SURREAL_DATABASE`, `SURREAL_USERNAME`, `SURREAL_PASSWORD`
- Auth client initialized in `dependencies.ts` via `createAuth(surreal, config)`

**Workspace Access Control:**
- `member_of` relation exists (TYPE RELATION IN person OUT workspace) per IAM documentation
- Chat routes do NOT check if requester is member of requested workspace
- No middleware in `start-server.ts` that validates session before route handlers

**Person Identity Model (schema):**
- `identities` field: array of `{provider: string, id: string}` for OAuth provider linking
- GitHub OAuth populates account table with provider_id="github", access_token stored encrypted
- Account linking tested (extract `0001_agent_session_and_subtask.surql` + OAuth integration tests in recent commits)

**Migration Sequence:**
- Latest: `0010_better_auth_tables.surql` (creates session, account, verification for better-auth)
- Next migration should use `0011_*` prefix

---

### Critical Gaps Identified

1. **No Session Middleware** — Chat routes completely unauthenticated. Workspace parameter is trusted client input.
2. **No Authority Check** — Person name resolution in extraction doesn't verify if resolver is workspace member.
3. **No Token Validation** — Better-auth is configured but never called to validate session in HTTP routes.
4. **IAM Phase 1 Incomplete** — Schema & better-auth integrated, but enforcement layer missing (Phase 2 per iam/README.md).

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the test infrastructure. Let me create a detailed summary report.

## Test Infrastructure Analysis Summary

### Key Findings:

**1. Smoke Test Pattern (smoke-test-kit.ts)**
- Uses `setupSmokeSuite(suiteName)` to create isolated namespaces/databases per test run
- Each test gets unique `namespace` (e.g., `smoke_${timestamp}_${random}`) and `database` (e.g., `${suite}_${random}`)
- Runtime spawns isolated Bun server process with test env vars (`SURREAL_NAMESPACE`, `SURREAL_DATABASE`)
- Full schema + migrations applied in `beforeAll`; cleanup in `afterAll` (unless `SMOKE_KEEP_DB` env var set)
- Provides `SmokeTestRuntime` with: `baseUrl`, `surreal` client, `namespace`, `database`, `port`

**2. Better-Auth Adapter Tests (better-auth-adapter.test.ts)**
- Direct integration test without HTTP layer — uses `betterAuth()` instance with `surrealdbAdapter(surreal)`
- Tests isolated namespace/database pattern (same as smoke tests)
- Creates custom SurrealDB v2 adapter that:
  - Maps field names (e.g., `contact_email` for `email`, `person_id` for `userId`)
  - Converts FK values to `RecordId` objects for writes
  - Returns plain strings from reads (better-auth contract)
  - Handles `null` → omitted fields conversion (project no-null rule)
- Comprehensive OAuth flow tests:
  - OAuth redirect URL generation
  - Account creation with provider fields
  - **Account linking** to existing email/password persons (lines 485-529)
  - `listUserAccounts()` queries

**3. MCP Auth Pattern (mcp/auth.ts)**
- Separate authentication layer for agent tools (not web chat)
- Uses Bearer token validation against workspace API key hashes
- Returns `McpAuthResult` with `workspaceRecord` + `workspaceName`
- 3 error cases: missing header (401), workspace not found (404), no API key configured (403), invalid key (401)

**4. Workspace Creation & Scope (workspace-routes.ts + workspace-scope.ts)**
- `handleCreateWorkspace()` creates workspace + owner person + onboarding conversation in transaction
- `resolveWorkspaceRecord()` validates workspace exists (404 if not)
- `resolveWorkspaceProjectRecord()` validates project linked to workspace (400 if not)
- Graph scope enforced via `has_project` edges (prevents cross-workspace access)

### Testing Patterns for IAM Implementation:

**Authority Enforcement Tests Should:**
1. Create isolated namespace/database with `setupSmokeSuite()`
2. Create workspace + person + workspace membership edge in transaction
3. Test API key generation/validation via `authenticateMcpRequest()`
4. Test workspace scope validation via `resolveWorkspaceRecord()` + `resolveWorkspaceProjectRecord()`
5. Test OAuth account linking like lines 485-529 of better-auth-adapter.test.ts
6. Verify `member_of` edges block cross-workspace access (not currently enforced in code)

**Key Files:**
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/tests/smoke/smoke-test-kit.ts` — test bootstrap
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/tests/smoke/better-auth-adapter.test.ts` — auth flow examples
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/mcp/auth.ts` — MCP auth enforcement
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/workspace/workspace-routes.ts` — workspace creation/scope
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/auth/adapter.ts` — field mapping, RecordId handling

**Schema Awareness:** Test setup applies full `/schema/surreal-schema.surql` including `person`, `session`, `account`, `workspace`, `member_of` (TYPE RELATION) tables.

# IAM Phase 1 Implementation Plan

## Architecture Overview

The implementation introduces a new `app/src/server/iam/` module that both chat tools and MCP routes import. The core pattern: a `checkAuthority()` function queries an `authority_scope` table in SurrealDB, and two wrappers adapt it for the chat tool path (`requireAuthorizedContext`) and the MCP route path (`checkAuthorityOrError`).

### Key Design Decisions

**Q1: Standalone module vs. tool helpers?**
Standalone at `app/src/server/iam/authority.ts`. Both chat tools and MCP routes need it, so it must live outside both directories.

**Q2: Wrapper vs. per-tool check?**
Wrapper. A new `requireAuthorizedContext(options, action, deps)` replaces `requireToolContext(options)` in write tools. Each tool declares its action as a string literal. Read-only tools keep using `requireToolContext()` unchanged.

**Q3: MCP path enforcement?**
Shared authority module. After `requireAuth()` succeeds, each MCP write handler calls `checkAuthority()` then `checkAuthorityOrError()` before any database mutation. Returns 403 Response on denial.

**Q4: Shared module?**
Yes. `app/src/server/iam/authority.ts` exports all three functions. Both chat tools and MCP routes import from it.

---

## Phase 1a: Foundation (Types + Schema + checkAuthority)

### Step 1: Extend ChatToolExecutionContext

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/chat/tools/types.ts`

Add three optional fields to `ChatToolExecutionContext`:

```typescript
import { RecordId, Surreal } from "surrealdb";

export type AgentType = "code_agent" | "architect" | "management" | "design_partner" | "observer";

export type ChatToolExecutionContext = {
  actor: "chat_agent" | "mcp" | "pm_agent" | "analytics_agent";
  agentType?: AgentType;                          // NEW
  humanPresent?: boolean;                          // NEW
  personRecord?: RecordId<"person", string>;       // NEW
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  conversationRecord: RecordId<"conversation", string>;
  currentMessageRecord: RecordId<"message", string>;
  latestUserText: string;
  workspaceOwnerRecord?: RecordId<"person", string>;
};

// ChatToolDeps and ChatAgentToolDeps unchanged
```

Re-export `AgentType` from `types.ts` so downstream consumers don't need a separate import.

### Step 2: authority_scope Schema + Seed Migration

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/schema/migrations/0011_authority_scope.surql`

```sql
BEGIN TRANSACTION;

DEFINE TABLE authority_scope SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD agent_type ON authority_scope TYPE string
  ASSERT $value IN ["code_agent", "architect", "management", "design_partner", "observer"];
DEFINE FIELD action ON authority_scope TYPE string
  ASSERT $value IN [
    "create_decision", "confirm_decision",
    "create_task", "complete_task",
    "create_observation", "acknowledge_observation", "resolve_observation",
    "create_question", "create_suggestion"
  ];
DEFINE FIELD permission ON authority_scope TYPE string
  ASSERT $value IN ["auto", "provisional", "propose", "blocked"];
DEFINE FIELD workspace ON authority_scope TYPE option<record<workspace>>;
DEFINE FIELD created_at ON authority_scope TYPE datetime;

DEFINE INDEX authority_scope_lookup ON authority_scope
  FIELDS agent_type, action, workspace UNIQUE;

-- Seed: code_agent (9 rows)
CREATE authority_scope CONTENT { agent_type: "code_agent", action: "create_decision", permission: "provisional", created_at: time::now() };
CREATE authority_scope CONTENT { agent_type: "code_agent", action: "confirm_decision", permission: "blocked", created_at: time::now() };
CREATE authority_scope CONTENT { agent_type: "code_agent", action: "create_task", permission: "auto", created_at: time::now() };
CREATE authority_scope CONTENT { agent_type: "code_agent", action: "complete_task", permission: "auto", created_at: time::now() };

### Step 3: checkAuthority Module

**New file:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/iam/authority.ts`

```typescript
import type { ToolExecutionOptions } from "ai";
import { RecordId, type Surreal } from "surrealdb";
import type { AgentType, ChatToolExecutionContext } from "../chat/tools/types";
import { requireToolContext } from "../chat/tools/helpers";
import { jsonError } from "../http/response";

// ---- Types ----

export type AuthorityAction =
  | "create_decision" | "confirm_decision"
  | "create_task" | "complete_task"
  | "create_observation" | "acknowledge_observation" | "resolve_observation"
  | "create_question" | "create_suggestion";

export type AuthorityPermission = "auto" | "provisional" | "propose" | "blocked";

export type AuthorizedContext = {
  context: ChatToolExecutionContext;
  permission: AuthorityPermission;
};

export class AuthorityError extends Error {
  constructor(
    public readonly permission: "propose" | "blocked",
    public readonly action: AuthorityAction,
    public readonly agentType: AgentType,
  ) {
    const verb = permission === "blocked" ? "not authorized" : "can only propose";
    super(`${agentType} is ${verb} for action: ${action}`);
    this.name = "AuthorityError";
  }
}

// ---- Actor-to-AgentType mapping ----

const ACTOR_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE: Partial<Record<string, AgentType>> = {
  pm_agent: "management",
  analytics_agent: "observer",
};

// ---- Core lookup ----

export async function checkAuthority(input: {
  surreal: Surreal;
  agentType: AgentType;
  action: AuthorityAction;
  workspaceRecord?: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
}): Promise<AuthorityPermission> {
  // 1. Try workspace-specific override
  if (input.workspaceRecord) {
    const [rows] = await input.surreal
      .query<[Array<{ permission: string }>]>(
        "SELECT permission FROM authority_scope WHERE agent_type = $agentType AND action = $action AND workspace = $workspace LIMIT 1;",
        {
          agentType: input.agentType,
          action: input.action,
          workspace: input.workspaceRecord,
        },
      )
      .collect<[Array<{ permission: string }>]>();

    if (rows.length > 0) {
      return rows[0].permission as AuthorityPermission;
    }
  }

  // 2. Fall back to global default (workspace IS NONE)
  const [rows] = await input.surreal
    .query<[Array<{ permission: string }>]>(
      "SELECT permission FROM authority_scope WHERE agent_type = $agentType AND action = $action AND workspace IS NONE LIMIT 1;",
      { agentType: input.agentType, action: input.action },
    )
    .collect<[Array<{ permission: string }>]>();

  if (rows.length > 0) {
    return rows[0].permission as AuthorityPermission;
  }

  // 3. Fail-safe: no row = blocked
  return "blocked";
}

// ---- Chat tool wrapper ----

export async function requireAuthorizedContext(
  options: ToolExecutionOptions,
  action: AuthorityAction,
  deps: { surreal: Surreal },
): Promise<AuthorizedContext> {
  const context = requireToolContext(options);

  // Human-present web sessions bypass authority (human IS the authority)
  if (context.humanPresent) {
    return { context, permission: "auto" };
  }

  const agentType: AgentType =
    context.agentType
    ?? ACTOR_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE[context.actor]
    ?? "code_agent";

  const permission = await checkAuthority({
    surreal: deps.surreal,
    agentType,
    action,
    workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
  });

  if (permission === "blocked") {
    throw new AuthorityError("blocked", action, agentType);
  }

  if (permission === "propose") {
    throw new AuthorityError("propose", action, agentType);
  }

  // "auto" or "provisional" - caller proceeds (may mark output as provisional)
  return { context, permission };
}

// ---- MCP route helper ----

export function checkAuthorityOrError(
  permission: AuthorityPermission,
  action: AuthorityAction,
  agentType: AgentType,
): Response | undefined {
  if (permission === "blocked") {
    return jsonError(`${agentType} is not authorized for action: ${action}`, 403);
  }
  if (permission === "propose") {
    return jsonError(
      `${agentType} can only propose ${action} -- requires human approval`,
      403,
    );
  }
  return undefined;
}
```

## Phase 1b: Authority Enforcement in Tools + MCP Routes

### Step 4a: Fix PM Agent Actor Propagation

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/agents/pm/agent.ts`

In `runPmAgent()`, override the actor when passing context:

```typescript
// BEFORE:
experimental_context: input.context,

// AFTER:
experimental_context: { ...input.context, actor: "pm_agent" as const },
```

This ensures PM agent tools resolve to `agentType: "management"` via the mapping table.

### Step 4b: Add Authority Checks to Write Tools

Each write tool changes from `requireToolContext(options)` to `requireAuthorizedContext(options, action, deps)`. The pattern for each tool:

```typescript
// BEFORE (e.g., create-provisional-decision.ts):
const context = requireToolContext(options);

// AFTER:
const { context, permission } = await requireAuthorizedContext(
  options, "create_decision", deps,
);
// If permission === "provisional", the decision is already created as provisional (matches existing behavior)
```

**Tool-to-action mapping:**

| Tool file | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| `create-provisional-decision.ts` | `create_decision` |
| `confirm-decision.ts` | `confirm_decision` (KEEP existing actor check too) |
| `create-observation.ts` | `create_observation` |
| `acknowledge-observation.ts` | `acknowledge_observation` |
| `resolve-observation.ts` | `resolve_observation` |
| `create-question.ts` | `create_question` |
| `create-work-item.ts` | `create_task` (for task/feature), `create_suggestion` (for project) -- or simplify: always `create_task` |
| `suggest-work-items.ts` | `create_suggestion` |
| `create-suggestion.ts` | `create_suggestion` |

**Files to modify** (all under `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/chat/tools/`):
- `create-provisional-decision.ts`
- `confirm-decision.ts`
- `create-observation.ts`
- `acknowledge-observation.ts`
- `resolve-observation.ts`
- `create-question.ts`
- `create-work-item.ts`
- `suggest-work-items.ts`
- `create-suggestion.ts`

For `confirm-decision.ts`, keep BOTH checks -- authority check first (blocks non-human programmatic callers), then the existing `actor !== "chat_agent"` check (ensures only the top-level chat agent, not subagents, can confirm).

### Step 4c: Add Authority Checks to MCP Routes

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/mcp/mcp-route.ts`

Add authority check before each write operation. The MCP auth result carries `agentType` (see Step 6). Pattern:

```typescript
// In handleCreateDecision:
async function handleCreateDecision(workspaceId: string, request: Request): Promise<Response> {
  const auth = await requireAuth(request, workspaceId);
  if (auth instanceof Response) return auth;

  // NEW: authority check
  const permission = await checkAuthority({
    surreal,
    agentType: auth.agentType,
    action: "create_decision",
    workspaceRecord: auth.workspaceRecord,
  });
  const denied = checkAuthorityOrError(permission, "create_decision", auth.agentType);
  if (denied) return denied;

  // ... existing logic, but if permission === "provisional", force status to "provisional"
}
```

**MCP handlers requiring authority checks:**

| Handler | Action |
|---------|--------|
| `handleCreateDecision` | `create_decision` |
| `handleAskQuestion` | `create_question` |
| `handleLogObservation` | `create_observation` |
| `handleCreateSubtask` | `create_task` |
| `handleUpdateTaskStatus` | `complete_task` |
| `handleSuggestEntity` | `create_suggestion` |
| `handleAcceptSuggestion` | `create_task` |
| `handleConvertSuggestion` | varies by `convert_to` target |
| `handleDismissSuggestion` | no write -- read-only, skip |
| `handleDeferSuggestion` | no write -- read-only, skip |

## Phase 1d: Identity Resolution

### Step 7: Identity Resolution Functions

**New file:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/iam/identity.ts`

```typescript
import { RecordId, type Surreal } from "surrealdb";

export async function resolveIdentity(input: {
  surreal: Surreal;
  provider: string;
  providerId: string;
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
}): Promise<RecordId<"person", string> | undefined> {
  const [rows] = await input.surreal
    .query<[Array<{ id: RecordId<"person", string> }>]>(
      [
        "SELECT id FROM person",
        "WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE `in` FROM member_of WHERE out = $workspace)",
        "AND identities[WHERE provider = $provider AND id = $providerId]",
        "LIMIT 1;",
      ].join(" "),
      {
        workspace: input.workspaceRecord,
        provider: input.provider,
        providerId: input.providerId,
      },
    )
    .collect<[Array<{ id: RecordId<"person", string> }>]>();

  return rows.length > 0 ? rows[0].id : undefined;
}

export async function resolveByEmail(input: {
  surreal: Surreal;
  email: string;
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
}): Promise<RecordId<"person", string> | undefined> {
  const normalizedEmail = input.email.trim().toLowerCase();
  if (normalizedEmail.length === 0) return undefined;

  const [rows] = await input.surreal
    .query<[Array<{ id: RecordId<"person", string> }>]>(
      [
        "SELECT id FROM person",
        "WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE `in` FROM member_of WHERE out = $workspace)",
        "AND string::lowercase(contact_email) = $email",
        "LIMIT 1;",
      ].join(" "),
      {
        workspace: input.workspaceRecord,
        email: normalizedEmail,
      },
    )
    .collect<[Array<{ id: RecordId<"person", string> }>]>();

  return rows.length > 0 ? rows[0].id : undefined;
}
```

### Step 8: Wire Into Extraction Pipeline

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/extraction/person.ts`

Extend `findWorkspacePersonByName` with email fallback, or create a new composite resolver:

```typescript
import { resolveByEmail } from "../iam/identity";

export async function resolveWorkspacePerson(input: {
  surreal: Surreal;
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  personName: string;
}): Promise<RecordId<"person", string> | undefined> {
  // 1. Exact name match (existing logic)
  const byName = await findWorkspacePersonByName(input);
  if (byName) return byName;

  // 2. Email match (if personName looks like an email)
  if (input.personName.includes("@")) {
    const byEmail = await resolveByEmail({
      surreal: input.surreal,
      email: input.personName,
      workspaceRecord: input.workspaceRecord,
    });
    if (byEmail) return byEmail;
  }

  return undefined;
}
```

Then update `applyAssigneeReference` in the extraction persist module to call `resolveWorkspacePerson` instead of `findWorkspacePersonByName`.

## Risk Areas and Mitigations

1. **PM agent inherits chat_agent's actor**: Fixed in Step 4a by overriding the actor field when passing context to `runPmAgent`. Must also apply the same fix for the analytics agent invocation if it uses shared tools.

2. **MCP route coverage**: 10+ handlers need individual authority checks. A missing check is a security gap. Mitigation: grep for all `createDecisionRecord`, `createObservation`, `createQuestionRecord`, `createSuggestion`, `createSubtask`, `updateTaskStatus` calls in mcp-route.ts and verify each has a preceding authority check.

3. **SurrealDB query for NONE workspace**: The query `WHERE workspace IS NONE` should correctly match rows where workspace was omitted (stored as NONE in SCHEMAFULL). Verified by the fact that `option<record<workspace>>` defaults to NONE when not set, and SurrealDB's `IS NONE` operator checks this.

4. **better-auth session resolution performance**: `auth.api.getSession()` hits the database on every chat request. Mitigation: better-auth has built-in session caching. If needed, add a TTL cache in a follow-up.

5. **Backwards compatibility**: All new fields on ChatToolExecutionContext are optional (`?`). Existing tools that don't use authority checks continue to work unchanged. The `humanPresent` field defaults to `undefined` (falsy), which means authority checks run -- this is the safe default (deny unless explicitly human).

    22→  }
    23→
    24→  if (normalized.includes(":")) {
    25→    const [table, id] = normalized.split(":", 2);
    26→    if (table !== "decision" || !id) {
    27→      throw new Error("decision id must reference a decision record");
    28→    }
    29→    return new RecordId("decision", id);
    30→  }
    31→
    32→  return new RecordId("decision", normalized);
    33→}
    34→

     1→import { tool } from "ai";
     2→import { z } from "zod";
     3→import { confirmDecisionRecord, getDecisionRecordForWorkspace, getWorkspaceOwnerRecord } from "../../graph/queries";
     4→import { requireToolContext, toDecisionRecordId } from "./helpers";
     5→import type { ChatToolDeps } from "./types";
     6→
     7→const CONFIRMABLE_STATUSES = new Set(["provisional", "inferred"]);
     8→
     9→export function createConfirmDecisionTool(deps: ChatToolDeps) {
    10→  return tool({
    11→    description:
    12→      "Confirm a provisional or inferred decision. ONLY use this when the user explicitly approves a decision in conversation. Never call this without clear user authorization.",
    13→    inputSchema: z.object({
    14→      decision_id: z.string().min(1).describe("Decision record ID to confirm"),
    15→      notes: z.string().optional().describe("Additional user context"),
    16→    }),
    17→    execute: async (input, options) => {
    18→      const context = requireToolContext(options);
    19→
    20→      if (context.actor !== "chat_agent") {
    21→        throw new Error("confirm_decision is only available for chat_agent context");
    22→      }
    23→
    24→      const decisionRecord = toDecisionRecordId(input.decision_id);
    25→      const decision = await getDecisionRecordForWorkspace({
    26→        surreal: deps.surreal,
    27→        workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
    28→        decisionInput: `decision:${decisionRecord.id as string}`,
    29→      });
    30→
    31→      if (!CONFIRMABLE_STATUSES.has(decision.status)) {
    32→        throw new Error(`decision is not confirmable from status '${decision.status}'`);
    33→      }
    34→
    35→      const ownerRecord = context.workspaceOwnerRecord
    36→        ? context.workspaceOwnerRecord
    37→        : await getWorkspaceOwnerRecord({
    38→            surreal: deps.surreal,
    39→            workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
    40→          });
    41→
    42→      const confirmedAt = new Date();
    43→      await confirmDecisionRecord({
    44→        surreal: deps.surreal,
    45→        decisionRecord: decision.id,
    46→        confirmedAt,
    47→        ...(ownerRecord ? { confirmedBy: ownerRecord } : {}),
    48→        ...(input.notes ? { notes: input.notes } : {}),
    49→      });
    50→
    51→      return {
    52→        decision_id: `decision:${decision.id.id as string}`,
    53→        status: "confirmed",
    54→        name: decision.summary,
    55→      };
    56→    },
    57→  });
    58→}
    59→

     1→import { RecordId, type Surreal } from "surrealdb";
     2→import { verifyApiKey } from "./api-key";
     3→import { jsonError } from "../http/response";
     4→
     5→type WorkspaceRow = {
     6→  id: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
     7→  name: string;
     8→  api_key_hash?: string;
     9→};
    10→
    11→export type McpAuthResult = {
    12→  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
    13→  workspaceName: string;
    14→};
    15→
    16→/**
    17→ * Authenticate an MCP request via Bearer token.
    18→ * Checks the Authorization header against workspace API key hashes.
    19→ * Returns workspace context on success, or an error Response.
    20→ */
    21→export async function authenticateMcpRequest(
    22→  request: Request,
    23→  workspaceId: string,
    24→  surreal: Surreal,
    25→): Promise<McpAuthResult | Response> {
    26→  const authHeader = request.headers.get("authorization");
    27→  if (!authHeader || !authHeader.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
    28→    return jsonError("missing or invalid Authorization header", 401);
    29→  }
    30→
    31→  const apiKey = authHeader.slice(7);
    32→  if (!apiKey) {
    33→    return jsonError("empty API key", 401);
    34→  }
    35→
    36→  const workspaceRecord = new RecordId("workspace", workspaceId);
    37→  const workspace = await surreal.select<WorkspaceRow>(workspaceRecord);
    38→  if (!workspace) {
    39→    return jsonError("workspace not found", 404);
    40→  }
    41→
    42→  if (!workspace.api_key_hash) {
    43→    return jsonError("workspace has no API key configured — run brain init", 403);
    44→  }
    45→
    46→  const valid = await verifyApiKey(apiKey, workspace.api_key_hash);
    47→  if (!valid) {
    48→    return jsonError("invalid API key", 401);
    49→  }
    50→
    51→  return {
    52→    workspaceRecord,
    53→    workspaceName: workspace.name,
    54→  };
    55→}
    56→
```

export async function processChatMessage(input: {
  deps: ServerDependencies;
  conversationId: string;
  messageId: string;
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  userMessageRecord: RecordId<"message", string>;
  userText: string;
  attachment?: IncomingAttachment;
  onboardingAction?: OnboardingAction;
}): Promise<void> {
  const startedAt = performance.now();
  logInfo("chat.message.process.execution.started", "Chat message processing execution started", {
    conversationId: input.conversationId,
    messageId: input.messageId,
    workspaceId: input.workspaceRecord.id as string,
    hasAttachment: input.attachment !== undefined,
  });

  try {
    const now = new Date();
    const conversationRecord = new RecordId("conversation", input.conversationId);
    const workspace = await input.deps.surreal.select<WorkspaceRow>(input.workspaceRecord);
    if (!workspace) {
      throw new Error("workspace not found");

  "content": "# IAM Phase 1: Authority Enforcement Implementation Plan\n\n## Context\n\nBetter-auth integration is complete (GitHub OAuth, session/account/verification tables, SurrealDB v2 adapter). But there's zero authorization enforcement — all tools execute unconditionally, chat routes have no session validation, and MCP routes only check API key existence (no per-action scoping). This plan implements the authority layer that gates who can do what.\n\n**Phase 2 compatibility:** The schema design supports workspace-specific overrides (nullable workspace field on authority_scope) so the Phase 2 \"authority config UI\" lands without schema changes. `checkAuthority` takes `agentType` as input (auth-method-agnostic) so OAuth 2.1 token resolution swaps in without touching enforcement logic.\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Extend ChatToolExecutionContext\n\n**File:** `app/src/server/chat/tools/types.ts`\n\nAdd `AgentType` union type and three optional fields to `ChatToolExecutionContext`:\n\n```typescript\nexport type AgentType = \"code_agent\" | \"architect\" | \"management\" | \"design_partner\" | \"observer\";\n```\n\nNew optional fields on `ChatToolExecutionContext`:\n- `agentType?: AgentType` — resolved agent type for authority lookups\n- `humanPresent?: boolean` — true for web chat (human at screen), false/undefined for MCP/background\n- `personRecord?: RecordId<\"person\", string>` — authenticated person (when session resolved)\n\nAll optional so existing code continues to work during incremental rollout.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: authority_scope Schema + Seed Migration\n\n**New file:** `schema/migrations/0011_authority_scope.surql`\n\nTable definition:\n```sql\nDEFINE TABLE authority_scope SCHEMAFULL;\nDEFINE FIELD agent_type ON authority_scope TYPE string;\nDEFINE FIELD action ON authority_scope TYPE string;\nDEFINE FIELD permission ON authority_scope TYPE string\n  ASSERT $value IN [\"auto\", \"provisional\", \"propose\", \"blocked\"];\nDEFINE FIELD workspace ON authority_scope TYPE option<record<workspace>>;\nDEFINE FIELD created_at ON authority_scope TYPE datetime;\nDEFINE INDEX authority_scope_lookup ON authority_scope\n  FIELDS agent_type, action, workspace UNIQUE;\n```\n\nSeed 45 rows (5 agent types x 9 actions) with `workspace` omitted (global defaults). Permission matrix:\n\n| Action | code_agent | architect | management | design_partner | observer |\n|--------|-----------|-----------|------------|----------------|----------|\n| create_decision | provisional | provisional | provisional | provisional | blocked |\n| confirm_decision | blocked | blocked | blocked | blocked | blocked |\n| create_task | auto | auto | auto | provisional | blocked |\n| complete_task | auto | blocked | auto | blocked | blocked |\n| create_observation | auto | auto | auto | auto | auto |\n| acknowledge_observation | auto | auto | auto | auto | blocked |\n| resolve_observation | blocked | auto | auto | blocked | blocked |\n| create_question | auto | auto | auto | auto | auto |\n| create_suggestion | auto | auto | auto | auto | blocked |\n\nAlso add the `DEFINE TABLE authority_scope` block to `schema/surreal-schema.surql` (the base schema for fresh DBs).\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Create IAM Authority Module\n\n**New file:** `app/src/server/iam/authority.ts`\n\nExports:\n\n### `checkAuthority(surreal, agentType, action, workspaceRecord?) -> AuthorityPermission`\n- Query workspace-specific row first: `WHERE agent_type=$at AND action=$act AND workspace=$ws`\n- Fallback to global: `WHERE agent_type=$at AND action=$act AND workspace IS NONE`\n- No match -> `\"blocked\"` (fail-safe)\n\n### `requireAuthorizedContext(options, action, deps) -> { context, permission }`\n- Calls `requireToolContext(options)` first (existing validation)\n- If `context.humanPresent === true` -> return `{ context, permission: \"auto\" }` (human IS the authority)\n- Resolve agentType: `context.agentType ?? ACTOR_DEFAULTS[context.actor] ?? \"code_agent\"`\n  - Actor defaults: `pm_agent -> \"management\"`, `analytics_agent -> \"observer\"`\n- Call `checkAuthority` -> throw `AuthorityError` for `\"blocked\"` or `\"propose\"`\n- Return `{ context, permission }` for `\"auto\"` or `\"provisional\"`\n\n### `checkAuthorityOrError(permission, action, agentType) -> Response | undefined`\n- MCP route helper: returns 403 Response for blocked/propose, undefined for auto/provisional\n\n### `AuthorityError` class\n- Carries `permission`, `action`, `agentType` for structured error handling\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Wire Authority Into Chat Tools\n\n### 4a: Fix PM agent actor propagation\n**File:** `app/src/server/agents/pm/agent.ts` (line 86)\n\nChange `experimental_context: input.context` to `experimental_context: { ...input.context, actor: \"pm_agent\" as const }` so PM tools resolve to `agentType: \"management\"`.\n\n### 4b: Add authority checks to write tools\n\nEach write tool replaces `requireToolContext(options)` with `requireAuthorizedContext(options, action, deps)`:\n\n| Tool file | Action |\n|-----------|--------|\n| `create-provisional-decision.ts` | `create-question.ts` | `\"create_question\"` |\n| `create-work-item.ts` | `\"create_task\"` |\n| `suggest-work-items.ts` | `\"create_suggestion\"` |\n| `create-suggestion.ts` | `\"create_suggestion\"`  workspaceRecord: RecordId<\"workspace\", string>;\n  workspaceName: string;\n  agentType: AgentType;  // NEW — from X-Agent-Type header, default \"code_agent\"\n};\n```\n\nIn each write handler's database call:\n\n| Handler (line) | Action |\n|----------------|--------|\n| `handleCreateProvisionalDecision` (583) | `\"create_decision\"` |\n| `handleResolveDecision` (429) | `handleUpdateTaskStatus` (692) | `\"complete_task\"` |\n| `handleConvertSuggestion` (1366) | `\"create_task\"` |\n\nPattern per handler:\n```typescript\nconst permission = await checkAuthority({ 84)\n\nAdd to `experimental_context`:\n```typescript\nhumanPresent: true,\n...(input.personRecord ? { personRecord: input.personRecord } : {}),\n```\n\n`humanPresent: true` for all web chat requests This preserves current behavior while enabling the enforcement layer for non-human paths.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Identity Resolution\n\n**New file:** `app/src/server/iam/identity.ts`\n\n### `resolveIdentity(surreal, provider, providerId, workspaceRecord) -> RecordId<\"person\"> | undefined`\nQuery person table for matching `identities[*].provider` + `identities[*].id` within workspace scope (via `member_of` edge).\n\n### `resolveByEmail(surreal, email, workspaceRecord) -> RecordId<\"person\"> | undefined`\nCase-insensitive `contact_email` match within workspace scope.\n\n**File:** `app/src/server/extraction/person.ts`\n\nAdd `resolveWorkspacePerson` that chains: exact name match (existing) -> email match (new, if input contains `@`). ## Approved Plan:
# IAM Phase 1: Authority Enforcement Implementation Plan

## Context

Better-auth integration is complete (GitHub OAuth, session/account/verification tables, SurrealDB v2 adapter). But there's zero authorization enforcement — all tools execute unconditionally, chat routes have no session validation, and MCP routes only check API key existence (no per-action scoping). This plan implements the authority layer that gates who can do what.

**Phase 2 compatibility:** The schema design supports workspace-specific overrides (nullable workspace field on authority_scope) so the Phase 2 "authority config UI" lands without schema changes. `checkAuthority` takes `agentType` as input (auth-method-agnostic) so OAuth 2.1 token resolution swaps in without touching enforcement logic.

---

## Step 1: Extend ChatToolExecutionContext

**File:** `app/src/server/chat/tools/types.ts`

Add `AgentType` union type and three optional fields to `ChatToolExecutionContext`:

```typescript
export type AgentType = "code_agent" | "architect" | "management" | "design_partner" | "observer";
```

New optional fields on `ChatToolExecutionContext`:
- `agentType?: AgentType` — resolved agent type for authority lookups
- `humanPresent?: boolean` — true for web chat (human at screen), false/undefined for MCP/background
- `personRecord?: RecordId<"person", string>` — authenticated person (when session resolved)

All optional so existing code continues to work during incremental rollout.

---

## Step 2: authority_scope Schema + Seed Migration

**New file:** `schema/migrations/0011_authority_scope.surql`

Table definition:
```sql
DEFINE TABLE authority_scope SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD agent_type ON authority_scope TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD action ON authority_scope TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD permission ON authority_scope TYPE string
  ASSERT $value IN ["auto", "provisional", "propose", "blocked"];
DEFINE FIELD workspace ON authority_scope TYPE option<record<workspace>>;
DEFINE FIELD created_at ON authority_scope TYPE datetime;
DEFINE INDEX authority_scope_lookup ON authority_scope
  FIELDS agent_type, action, workspace UNIQUE;
```

Seed 45 rows (5 agent types x 9 actions) with `workspace` omitted (global defaults). Permission matrix:

| Action | code_agent | architect | management | design_partner | observer |
|--------|-----------|-----------|------------|----------------|----------|
| create_decision | provisional | provisional | provisional | provisional | blocked |
| confirm_decision | blocked | blocked | blocked | blocked | blocked |
| create_task | auto | auto | auto | provisional | blocked |
| complete_task | auto | blocked | auto | blocked | blocked |
| create_observation | auto | auto | auto | auto | auto |
| acknowledge_observation | auto | auto | auto | auto | blocked |
| resolve_observation | blocked | auto | auto | blocked | blocked |
| create_question | auto | auto | auto | auto | auto |
| create_suggestion | auto | auto | auto | auto | blocked |

Also add the `DEFINE TABLE authority_scope` block to `schema/surreal-schema.surql` (the base schema for fresh DBs).

---

## Step 3: Create IAM Authority Module

**New file:** `app/src/server/iam/authority.ts`

Exports:

### `checkAuthority(surreal, agentType, action, workspaceRecord?) -> AuthorityPermission`
- Query workspace-specific row first: `WHERE agent_type=$at AND action=$act AND workspace=$ws`
- Fallback to global: `WHERE agent_type=$at AND action=$act AND workspace IS NONE`
- No match -> `"blocked"` (fail-safe)

### `requireAuthorizedContext(options, action, deps) -> { context, permission }`
- Calls `requireToolContext(options)` first (existing validation)
- If `context.humanPresent === true` -> return `{ context, permission: "auto" }` (human IS the authority)
- Resolve agentType: `context.agentType ?? ACTOR_DEFAULTS[context.actor] ?? "code_agent"`
  - Actor defaults: `pm_agent -> "management"`, `analytics_agent -> "observer"`
- Call `checkAuthority` -> throw `AuthorityError` for `"blocked"` or `"propose"`
- Return `{ context, permission }` for `"auto"` or `"provisional"`

### `checkAuthorityOrError(permission, action, agentType) -> Response | undefined`
- MCP route helper: returns 403 Response for blocked/propose, undefined for auto/provisional

### `AuthorityError` class
- Carries `permission`, `action`, `agentType` for structured error handling

---

## Step 4: Wire Authority Into Chat Tools

### 4a: Fix PM agent actor propagation
**File:** `app/src/server/agents/pm/agent.ts` (line 86)

Change `experimental_context: input.context` to `experimental_context: { ...input.context, actor: "pm_agent" as const }` so PM tools resolve to `agentType: "management"`.

### 4b: Add authority checks to write tools

Each write tool replaces `requireToolContext(options)` with `requireAuthorizedContext(options, action, deps)`:

| Tool file | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| `create-provisional-decision.ts` | `"create_decision"` |
| `confirm-decision.ts` | `"confirm_decision"` (keep existing actor check too) |
| `resolve-decision.ts` | `"create_decision"` (reuse — resolving is a decision mutation) |
| `create-observation.ts` | `"create_observation"` |
| `acknowledge-observation.ts` | `"acknowledge_observation"` |
| `resolve-observation.ts` | `"resolve_observation"` |
| `create-question.ts` | `"create_question"` |
| `create-work-item.ts` | `"create_task"` |
| `suggest-work-items.ts` | `"create_suggestion"` |
| `create-suggestion.ts` | `"create_suggestion"` |

Read-only tools (`search_entities`, `get_entity_detail`, `get_project_status`, `get_conversation_history`, `list_workspace_entities`, `show_relationship_graph`, `check_constraints`) keep using `requireToolContext` unchanged.

---

## Step 5: Wire Authority Into MCP Routes

**File:** `app/src/server/mcp/auth.ts`

Extend `McpAuthResult`:
```typescript
export type McpAuthResult = {
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
  workspaceName: string;
  agentType: AgentType;  // NEW — from X-Agent-Type header, default "code_agent"
};
```

In `authenticateMcpRequest`: read `X-Agent-Type` header with validation against allowed values, default to `"code_agent"`.

**File:** `app/src/server/mcp/mcp-route.ts`

Add authority check before each write handler's database call:

| Handler (line) | Action |
|----------------|--------|
| `handleCreateProvisionalDecision` (583) | `"create_decision"` |
| `handleResolveDecision` (429) | `"create_decision"` |
| `handleAskQuestion` (631) | `"create_question"` |
| `handleLogObservation` (794) | `"create_observation"` |
| `handleCreateSubtask` (721) | `"create_task"` |
| `handleUpdateTaskStatus` (692) | `"complete_task"` |
| `handleConvertSuggestion` (1366) | `"create_task"` |

Pattern per handler:
```typescript
const permission = await checkAuthority({ surreal, agentType: auth.agentType, action: "...", workspaceRecord: auth.workspaceRecord });
const denied = checkAuthorityOrError(permission, "...", auth.agentType);
if (denied) return denied;
```

---

## Step 6: Web Chat Session Auth

**File:** `app/src/server/chat/chat-ingress.ts`

Before calling `processChatMessage`, attempt session resolution (non-blocking — unauthenticated chat still works during transition):

```typescript
let personRecord: RecordId<"person", string> | undefined;
try {
  const session = await deps.auth.api.getSession({ headers: request.headers });
  if (session?.user?.id) {
    personRecord = new RecordId("person", session.user.id);
  }
} catch { /* no session — proceed without person */ }
```

Pass `personRecord` through to `processChatMessage`.

**File:** `app/src/server/chat/chat-processor.ts`

Thread `personRecord` through to `runChatAgent` input.

**File:** `app/src/server/chat/handler.ts` (line 84)

Add to `experimental_context`:
```typescript
humanPresent: true,
...(input.personRecord ? { personRecord: input.personRecord } : {}),
```

`humanPresent: true` for all web chat requests means authority checks return `"auto"` — the human at the screen IS the authority. This preserves current behavior while enabling the enforcement layer for non-human paths.

---

## Step 7: Identity Resolution

**New file:** `app/src/server/iam/identity.ts`

### `resolveIdentity(surreal, provider, providerId, workspaceRecord) -> RecordId<"person"> | undefined`
Query person table for matching `identities[*].provider` + `identities[*].id` within workspace scope (via `member_of` edge).

### `resolveByEmail(surreal, email, workspaceRecord) -> RecordId<"person"> | undefined`
Case-insensitive `contact_email` match within workspace scope.

**File:** `app/src/server/extraction/person.ts`

Add `resolveWorkspacePerson` that chains: exact name match (existing) -> email match (new, if input contains `@`). Update `applyAssigneeReference` callers to use the composite resolver.

---

{
  "file_path": "/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/riga/app/src/server/iam/authority.ts",
  "content": "import type { ToolExecutionOptions } from \"ai\";\nimport { RecordId, type Surreal } from \"surrealdb\";\nimport type { AgentType, ChatToolExecutionContext } from \"../chat/tools/types\";\nimport { requireToolContext } from \"../chat/tools/helpers\";\nimport { jsonError } from \"../http/response\";\n\nexport type AuthorityAction =\n  | \"create_decision\"\n  | \"confirm_decision\"\n  | \"create_task\"\n  | \"complete_task\"\n  | \"create_observation\"\n  | \"acknowledge_observation\"\n  | \"resolve_observation\"\n  | \"create_question\"\n  | \"create_suggestion\";\n\nexport type AuthorityPermission = \"auto\" | \"provisional\" | \"propose\" | \"blocked\";\n\nexport type AuthorizedContext = {\n  context: ChatToolExecutionContext;\n  permission: AuthorityPermission;\n};\n\nexport class AuthorityError extends Error {\n  constructor(\n    public readonly permission: \"propose\" | \"blocked\",\n    public readonly action: AuthorityAction,\n    public readonly agentType: AgentType,\n  ) {\n    const verb = permission === \"blocked\" ? \"not authorized\" : \"can only propose\";\n    super(`${agentType} is ${verb} for action: ${action}`);\n    this.name = \"AuthorityError\";\n  }\n}\n\nconst ACTOR_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE: Partial<Record<string, AgentType>> = {\n  pm_agent: \"management\",\n  analytics_agent: \"observer\",\n};\n\nexport async function checkAuthority(input: {\n  surreal: Surreal;\n  agentType: AgentType;\n  action: AuthorityAction;\n  workspaceRecord?: RecordId<\"workspace\", string>;\n}): Promise<AuthorityPermission> {\n  // 1. Try workspace-specific override\n  if (input.workspaceRecord) {\n    const [wsRows] = await input.surreal.query<[Array<{ permission: string }>]>(\n      \"SELECT permission FROM authority_scope WHERE agent_type = $agentType AND action = $action AND workspace = $workspace LIMIT 1;\",\n      {\n        agentType: input.agentType,\n        action: input.action,\n        workspace: input.workspaceRecord,\n      },\n    );\n\n    if (wsRows.length > 0) {\n      return wsRows[0].permission as AuthorityPermission;\n    }\n  }\n\n  // 2. Fall back to global default (workspace IS NONE)\n  const [globalRows] = await input.surreal.query<[Array<{ permission: string }>]>(\n    \"SELECT permission FROM authority_scope WHERE agent_type = $agentType AND action = $action AND workspace IS NONE LIMIT 1;\",\n    { agentType: input.agentType, action: input.action },\n  );\n\n  if (globalRows.length > 0) {\n    return globalRows[0].permission as AuthorityPermission;\n  }\n\n  // 3. Fail-safe: no row = blocked\n  return \"blocked\";\n}\n\nexport async function requireAuthorizedContext(\n  options: ToolExecutionOptions,\n  action: AuthorityAction,\n  deps: { surreal: Surreal },\n): Promise<AuthorizedContext> {\n  const context = requireToolContext(options);\n\n  // Human-present web sessions bypass authority (human IS the authority)\n  if (context.humanPresent) {\n    return { context, permission: \"auto\" };\n  }\n\n  const agentType: AgentType =\n    context.agentType ?? ACTOR_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE[context.actor] ?? \"code_agent\";\n\n  const permission = await checkAuthority({\n    surreal: deps.surreal,\n    agentType,\n    action,\n    workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,\n  });\n\n  if (permission === \"blocked\") {\n    throw new AuthorityError(\"blocked\", action, agentType);\n  }\n\n  if (permission === \"propose\") {\n    throw new AuthorityError(\"propose\", action, agentType);\n  }\n\n  // \"auto\" or \"provisional\" — caller proceeds (may mark output as provisional)\n  return { context, permission };\n}\n\nexport function checkAuthorityOrError(\n  permission: AuthorityPermission,\n  action: AuthorityAction,\n  agentType: AgentType,\n): Response | undefined {\n  if (permission === \"blocked\") {\n    return jsonError(`${agentType} is not authorized for action: ${action}`, 403);\n  }\n  if (permission === \"propose\") {\n    return jsonError(\n      `${agentType} can only propose ${action} — requires human approval`,\n      403,\n    );\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n"
}

    12→    rationale: z.string().min(1),
    13→    category: z.enum(["none", ...ENTITY_CATEGORIES]),
    14→    project: z.enum(["none"]).or(z.string().min(1)),
    15→    priority: z.enum(["none", ...ENTITY_PRIORITIES]),
    16→    possible_duplicate: z.enum(["none"]).or(
    17→      z.object({
    18→        id: z.string().min(1),
    19→        name: z.string().min(1),
    20→        similarity: z.number().min(0).max(1),
    21→      }),
    22→    ),
    23→  })
    24→  .transform((v) => ({
    25→    ...v,
    26→    category: v.category === "none" ? undefined : v.category,
    27→    project: v.project === "none" ? undefined : v.project,
    28→    priority: v.priority === "none" ? undefined : v.priority,
    29→    possible_duplicate: v.possible_duplicate === "none" ? undefined : v.possible_duplicate,
    30→  }));
    31→
    32→const pmAgentResultSchema = z.object({
    33→  summary: z.string().min(1),
    34→  suggestions: z.array(workItemSuggestionSchema),
    35→  updated: z.array(
    36→    z.object({
    37→      existing_id: z.string().min(1),
    38→      title: z.string().min(1),
    39→      changes: z.string().min(1),
    40→    }),
    41→  ),
    42→  discarded: z.array(
    43→    z.object({
    44→      title: z.string().min(1),
    45→      reason: z.string().min(1),
    46→    }),
    47→  ),
    48→  observations_created: z.array(z.string().min(1)),
    49→});
    50→
    51→export type WorkItemSuggestion = z.infer<typeof workItemSuggestionSchema>;
    52→export type PmAgentResult = z.infer<typeof pmAgentResultSchema>;
    53→
    54→export type PmAgentOutput = PmAgentResult & {
    55→  extracted_entities: ExtractedEntity[];
    56→  extracted_relationships: ExtractedRelationship[];
    57→};
    58→
    59→export type PmAgentInput = {
    60→  deps: ChatToolDeps & { pmAgentModel: any };
    61→  context: ChatToolExecutionContext;
    62→  intent: "plan_work" | "check_status" | "organize" | "track_dependencies";
    63→  conversationContext: string;
    64→  project?: string;
    65→};
    66→
    67→const INTENT_INSTRUCTIONS: Record<PmAgentInput["intent"], string> = {
    68→  check_status: "Primary action: call get_project_status when a project scope is available.",
    69→  plan_work: "Primary action: propose tasks/features/projects with suggest_work_items or create_work_item, deduping each item.",
    70→  track_dependencies: "Primary action: identify blockers/dependencies and create observations for high-risk paths.",
    71→  organize: "Primary action: organize work into clear, deduplicated next steps.",
    72→};
    73→
    74→export async function runPmAgent(input: PmAgentInput): Promise<PmAgentOutput> {
    75→  const system = await buildPmSystemPrompt({
    76→    surreal: input.deps.surreal,
    77→    workspaceRecord: input.context.workspaceRecord,
    78→  });
    79→
    80→  const agent = new ToolLoopAgent({
    81→    id: "pm-agent",
    82→    model: input.deps.pmAgentModel,
    83→    instructions: system,
    84→    tools: createPmTools(input.deps),
    85→    output: Output.object({ schema: pmAgentResultSchema }),
    86→    experimental_context: input.context,
    87→    stopWhen: stepCountIs(6),
    88→  });
    89→
    90→  const result = await agent.generate({
    91→    prompt: [
    92→      "You are handling a PM request.",
    93→      `Intent: ${input.intent}`,
    94→      input.project ? `Project hint: ${input.project}` : "Project hint: not provided",
    95→      INTENT_INSTRUCTIONS[input.intent],
    96→      "Context:",
    97→      input.conversationContext,
    98→    ].join("\n"),
    99→  });
   100→
   101→  if (!result.output) {
   102→    throw new Error("pm agent did not produce structured output");
   103→  }
   104→
   105→  const extracted_entities: ExtractedEntity[] = [];
   106→  const extracted_relationships: ExtractedRelationship[] = [];
   107→
   108→  for (const step of result.steps) {
   109→    for (const toolResult of step.toolResults) {
   110→      const res = toolResult.output as Record<string, unknown> | undefined;
   111→      if (res) {
   112→        if (Array.isArray(res.extracted_entities)) {
   113→          extracted_entities.push(...(res.extracted_entities as ExtractedEntity[]));
   114→        }
   115→        if (Array.isArray(res.extracted_relationships)) {
   116→          extracted_relationships.push(...(res.extracted_relationships as ExtractedRelationship[]));
   117→        }
   118→      }
   119→    }
   120→  }
   121→
   122→  return {
   123→    ...result.output,
   124→    extracted_entities,
   125→    extracted_relationships,
   126→  };
   127→}
   128→

     1→import { tool } from "ai";
     2→import { z } from "zod";
     3→import {
     4→  createDecisionRecord,
     5→  createExtractionProvenanceEdge,
     6→  resolveWorkspaceFeatureRecord,
     7→  resolveWorkspaceProjectRecord,
     8→} from "../../graph/queries";
     9→import { requireToolContext } from "./helpers";
    10→import type { ChatToolDeps } from "./types";
    11→
    12→export function createCreateProvisionalDecisionTool(deps: ChatToolDeps) {
    13→  return tool({
    14→    description:
    15→      "Create a provisional decision when no existing answer exists. Use after resolve_decision returns unresolved and the user wants to proceed.",
    16→    inputSchema: z.object({
    17→      name: z.string().min(1).describe("Concise decision name"),
    18→      rationale: z.string().min(1).describe("Why this decision was made"),
    19→      context: z
    20→        .object({
    21→          project: z.string().optional(),
    22→          feature: z.string().optional(),
    23→        })
    24→        .optional(),
    25→      options_considered: z.array(z.string().min(1)).optional(),
    26→    }),
    27→    execute: async (input, options) => {
    28→      const context = requireToolContext(options);
    29→      const now = new Date();
    30→
    31→      const projectRecord = input.context?.project
    32→        ? await resolveWorkspaceProjectRecord({
    33→            surreal: deps.surreal,
    34→            workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
    35→            projectInput: input.context.project,
    36→          })
    37→        : undefined;
    38→
    39→      const featureRecord = input.context?.feature
    40→        ? await resolveWorkspaceFeatureRecord({
    41→            surreal: deps.surreal,
    42→            workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
    43→            featureInput: input.context.feature,
    44→          })
    45→        : undefined;
    46→
    47→      const decisionRecord = await createDecisionRecord({
    48→        surreal: deps.surreal,
    49→        summary: input.name,
    50→        status: "provisional",
    51→        now,
    52→        workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
    53→        sourceMessageRecord: context.currentMessageRecord,
    54→        rationale: input.rationale,
    55→        ...(input.options_considered && input.options_considered.length > 0
    56→          ? { optionsConsidered: input.options_considered }
    57→          : {}),
    58→        decidedByName: "chat_agent",
    59→        ...(projectRecord ? { projectRecord } : {}),
    60→        ...(featureRecord ? { featureRecord } : {}),
    61→      });
    62→
    63→      await createExtractionProvenanceEdge({
    64→        surreal: deps.surreal,
    65→        sourceRecord: context.currentMessageRecord,
    66→        targetRecord: decisionRecord,
    67→        now,
    68→        confidence: 0.9,
    69→        model: deps.extractionModelId,
    70→        fromText: input.name,
    71→        evidence: context.latestUserText,
    72→        evidenceSourceRecord: context.currentMessageRecord,
    73→      });
    74→
    75→      return {
    76→        decision_id: `decision:${decisionRecord.id as string}`,
    77→        status: "provisional",
    78→        review_required: true,
    79→      };
    80→    },
    81→  });
    82→}
    83→

     1→import { tool } from "ai";
     2→import { RecordId } from "surrealdb";
     3→import { z } from "zod";
     4→import { ENTITY_CATEGORIES } from "../../../shared/contracts";
     5→import { createEmbeddingVector } from "../../graph/embeddings";
     6→import { isEntityInWorkspace, parseRecordIdString } from "../../graph/queries";
     7→import { createObservation } from "../../observation/queries";
     8→import { requireToolContext } from "./helpers";
     9→import type { ChatToolDeps } from "./types";
    10→
    11→export function createCreateObservationTool(deps: ChatToolDeps) {
    12→  return tool({
    13→    description:
    14→      "Create an observation in the workspace graph. Use proactively when you notice cross-cutting concerns — risks, conflicts, gaps, or notable facts — even if the user didn't ask.",
    15→    inputSchema: z.object({
    16→      text: z.string().min(1).describe("Observation text"),
    17→      severity: z
    18→        .enum(["info", "warning", "conflict"])
    19→        .describe(
    20→          "info: awareness-level signals. warning: risks to address soon. conflict: contradictions needing human resolution.",
    21→        ),
    22→      category: z.enum(ENTITY_CATEGORIES).optional().describe("Optional observation category"),
    23→      related_entity_id: z
    24→        .string()
    25→        .optional()
    26→        .describe("Optional related entity id (project/feature/task/decision/question)"),
    27→    }),
    28→    execute: async (input, options) => {
    29→      const context = requireToolContext(options);
    30→
    31→      const relatedRecord = input.related_entity_id
    32→        ? parseRecordIdString(
    33→            input.related_entity_id,
    34→            ["project", "feature", "task", "decision", "question"],
    35→          ) as RecordId<"project" | "feature" | "task" | "decision" | "question", string>
    36→        : undefined;
    37→
    38→      if (relatedRecord) {
    39→        const scoped = await isEntityInWorkspace(deps.surreal, context.workspaceRecord, relatedRecord);
    40→        if (!scoped) {
    41→          throw new Error("related entity is outside the current workspace scope");
    42→        }
    43→      }
    44→
    45→      const embedding = await createEmbeddingVector(deps.embeddingModel, input.text, deps.embeddingDimension);
    46→      if (!embedding) {
    47→        throw new Error("failed to create embedding for create_observation");
    48→      }
    49→
    50→      const observationRecord = await createObservation({
    51→        surreal: deps.surreal,
    52→        workspaceRecord: context.workspaceRecord,
    53→        text: input.text,
    54→        severity: input.severity,
    55→        ...(input.category ? { category: input.category } : {}),
    56→        sourceAgent: context.actor,
    57→        now: new Date(),
    58→        sourceMessageRecord: context.currentMessageRecord,
    59→        ...(relatedRecord ? { relatedRecord } : {}),
    60→        embedding,
    61→      });
    62→
    63→      return {
    64→        observation_id: `observation:${observationRecord.id as string}`,
    65→        severity: input.severity,
    66→        status: "open",
    67→      };
    68→    },
    69→  });
    70→}
    71→
